Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Well, I came in because... it's my head. My mind just keeps going blank at the absolute worst times. Like, I'll be at work, and I'll look at the road-closure forms or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this for years. It's like the time just evaporates. Which reminds me, the other day, this guy in a blue Honda—he had this bumper sticker about saving the bees, which I think is so important because, you know, without bees, the whole ecosystem just collapses. Energized? No... no, I don't think so. Honestly, most of the time I just feel incredibly heavy. Like I'm walking through wet cement. I do try to drink coffee to get some kind of energy for my shifts, though. I read an article once about air quality and how it affects pets faster than humans because they breathe faster... or maybe it's because they're closer to the ground? I can't remember. My mom used to have a dog that had asthma, and they had to give it an inhaler, which just seems so strange to look at. 

But anyway... no. I never really get those big bursts of energy or feel high like you're saying. I'm usually just tired.

Trouble paying attention in school? I... I don't really remember having a problem with it. I was pretty quiet, mostly. I liked reading. I remember reading this one book about a girl who lived in a windmill, and she had to figure out how to grind the flour herself after her parents left. It was so detailed about the gears and the stones. I actually tried to build a little model windmill out of popsicle sticks and rubber bands for a science project after that. My dad helped me glue the base together because the glue gun was so hot. He used to keep all his tools in these old metal coffee cans in the garage, the kind that smelled like stale coffee and motor oil. He had this whole system for sorting the nails by size, but then my brother knocked one over once and they just went everywhere. It took us hours to pick them all up out of the sawdust on the floor. 

But no, I don't think I had trouble paying attention. I just kind of kept my head down and did my work.

Lose track of time? Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'll look at the clock on my phone, and it's 10:15, and then I blink, and my supervisor is tapping me on the shoulder to tell me it's time for my lunch break at noon. My phone actually has this really annoying crack right across the screen, right where the time is. I dropped it last week when I was trying to get my keys out of my pocket. My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I read that in a waiting room magazine once. It's fascinating how much we've learned about dinosaurs just from fossils. They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. 

But, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. I just don't know where it goes.

I don't really remember having a problem with it. I was pretty quiet, mostly. I liked reading. I remember reading this one book about a girl who lived in a windmill, and she had to figure out how to grind the flour herself after her parents left. It was so detailed about the gears and the stones. I actually tried to build a little model windmill out of popsicle sticks and rubber bands for a science project after that. My dad helped me glue the base together because the glue gun was so hot. He used to keep all his tools in these old metal coffee cans in the garage, the kind that smelled like stale coffee and motor oil. He had this whole system for sorting the nails by size, but then my brother knocked one over once and they just went everywhere. It took us hours to pick them all up out of the sawdust on the floor. \n\nBut no, I don't think I had trouble paying attention. I just kind of kept my head down and did my work.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you lose track of time?\n\nPatient: Lose track of time? Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'll look at the clock on my phone, and it's 10:15, and then I blink, and my supervisor is tapping me on the shoulder to tell me it's time for my lunch break at noon. My phone actually has this really annoying crack right across the screen, right where the time is. I dropped it last week when I was trying to get my keys out of my pocket. My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I read that in a waiting room magazine once. It's fascinating how much we've learned about dinosaurs just from fossils. They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. 

Like the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.

But... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. I don't know what's happening around me at all until I snap out of it and realize I don't know what I'm supposed to be doing with the paddle.

I... I don't really remember having a problem with it. I was pretty quiet, mostly. I liked reading. I remember reading this one book about a girl who lived in a windmill, and she had to figure out how to grind the flour herself after her parents left. It was so detailed about the gears and the stones. I actually tried to build a little model windmill out of popsicle sticks and rubber bands for a science project after that. My dad helped me glue the base together because the glue gun was so hot. He used to keep all his tools in these old metal coffee cans in the garage, the kind that smelled like stale coffee and motor oil. He had this whole system for sorting the nails by size, but then my brother knocked one over once and they just went everywhere. It took us hours to pick them all up out of the sawdust on the floor. \n\nBut no, I don't think I had trouble paying attention. I just kind of kept my head down and did my work.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you lose track of time?\n\nPatient: Lose track of time? Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'll look at the clock on my phone, and it's 10:15, and then I blink, and my supervisor is tapping me on the shoulder to tell me it's time for my lunch break at noon. My phone actually has this really annoying crack right across the screen, right where the time is. I dropped it last week when I was trying to get my keys out of my pocket. My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I just don't know where it goes.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, are you aware of what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. \n\nLike the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.\n\nBut... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. 

You know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. We have to use these special waterproof clipboards, the ones that have a little plastic cover that folds down over the paper. My supervisor, Dave, he lost his clipboard last month and had to use a regular one during a downpour. The forms practically dissolved. Dave is always losing things, though. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. 

But yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.

Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. 

Speaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. 

But, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.

I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Shake or jerk? No, no one's ever said anything like that to me. I think I just stand there, completely still. Like a statue. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. 

I did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. 

But no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.

chief_complaint_symptom_code: S031\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells ends, can you remember what happened during it?\n\nPatient: Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. \n\nYou know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells tend to happen when you’re under emotional stress?\n\nPatient: Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that you shake or have jerking movements during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Shake or jerk? No, no one's ever said anything like that to me. I think I just stand there, completely still. Like a statue. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\n[Last Question]\nRight before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. 

Speaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. 

But no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.

My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I read that in a waiting room magazine once. It's fascinating how much we've learned about dinosaurs just from fossils. They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. I just don't know where it goes.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, are you aware of what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. \n\nLike the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.\n\nBut... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. I don't know what's happening around me at all until I snap out of it and realize I don't know what I'm supposed to be doing with the paddle.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells ends, can you remember what happened during it?\n\nPatient: Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. \n\nYou know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. We have to use these special waterproof clipboards, the ones that have a little plastic cover that folds down over the paper. My supervisor, Dave, he lost his clipboard last month and had to use a regular one during a downpour. The forms practically dissolved. Dave is always losing things, though. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. \n\nBut yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells tend to happen when you’re under emotional stress?\n\nPatient: Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that you shake or have jerking movements during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Shake or jerk? Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nPatient: Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. \n\nSpeaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. My mom used to have a dog that had asthma, and they had to give it an inhaler, which just seems so strange to look at. \n\nBut anyway... no. I never really get those big bursts of energy or feel high like you're saying. I'm usually just tired.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did you often have trouble paying attention?\n\nPatient: Trouble paying attention in school? I... I don't really remember having a problem with it. I was pretty quiet, mostly. I liked reading. I remember reading this one book about a girl who lived in a windmill, and she had to figure out how to grind the flour herself after her parents left. It was so detailed about the gears and the stones. I actually tried to build a little model windmill out of popsicle sticks and rubber bands for a science project after that. My dad helped me glue the base together because the glue gun was so hot. He used to keep all his tools in these old metal coffee cans in the garage, the kind that smelled like stale coffee and motor oil. He had this whole system for sorting the nails by size, but then my brother knocked one over once and they just went everywhere. It took us hours to pick them all up out of the sawdust on the floor. \n\nBut no, I don't think I had trouble paying attention. I just kind of kept my head down and did my work.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you lose track of time?\n\nPatient: Lose track of time? Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'll look at the clock on my phone, and it's 10:15, and then I blink, and my supervisor is tapping me on the shoulder to tell me it's time for my lunch break at noon. My phone actually has this really annoying crack right across the screen, right where the time is. I dropped it last week when I was trying to get my keys out of my pocket. My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I read that in a waiting room magazine once. It's fascinating how much we've learned about dinosaurs just from fossils. They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. I just don't know where it goes.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, are you aware of what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. \n\nLike the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.\n\nBut... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. I don't know what's happening around me at all until I snap out of it and realize I don't know what I'm supposed to be doing with the paddle.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells ends, can you remember what happened during it?\n\nPatient: Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. \n\nYou know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. We have to use these special waterproof clipboards, the ones that have a little plastic cover that folds down over the paper. My supervisor, Dave, he lost his clipboard last month and had to use a regular one during a downpour. The forms practically dissolved. Dave is always losing things, though. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. \n\nBut yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells tend to happen when you’re under emotional stress?\n\nPatient: Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that you shake or have jerking movements during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Shake or jerk? No, no one's ever said anything like that to me. I think I just stand there, completely still. Like a statue. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nPatient: Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. \n\nSpeaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. \n\nBut no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever seen you do repetitive movements, like lip smacking or picking at things, during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. Although, my lips do get incredibly chapped out there on the road with all the wind and the dust from the construction. I have to carry this special beeswax lip balm in my safety vest pocket all the time. It has peppermint in it, which is supposed to be really soothing. Peppermint is great for headaches, too, you know. My aunt used to grow real peppermint in her garden. She had this massive garden in her backyard with all these wooden raised beds. She grew tomatoes that were the size of softballs, I swear. We used to make fresh salsa every single summer. I love a good salsa, but it absolutely has to have cilantro in it. Some people think cilantro tastes like soap, which I read somewhere is actually a genetic thing. I think genetics are so fascinating. Like how my sister and I both have the exact same freckle on our left wrist, right over the vein. \n\nBut, um... what was it? Picking at things? No. Nobody has ever said I do anything like that when my mind empties out. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nPatient: Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. \n\nSpeaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. \n\nBut no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever seen you do repetitive movements, like lip smacking or picking at things, during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. Although, my lips do get incredibly chapped out there on the road with all the wind and the dust from the construction. I have to carry this special beeswax lip balm in my safety vest pocket all the time. It has peppermint in it, which is supposed to be really soothing. Peppermint is great for headaches, too, you know. My aunt used to grow real peppermint in her garden. She had this massive garden in her backyard with all these wooden raised beds. She grew tomatoes that were the size of softballs, I swear. We used to make fresh salsa every single summer. I love a good salsa, but it absolutely has to have cilantro in it. Some people think cilantro tastes like soap, which I read somewhere is actually a genetic thing. I think genetics are so fascinating. Like how my sister and I both have the exact same freckle on our left wrist, right over the vein. \n\nBut, um... what was it? Picking at things? No. Nobody has ever said I do anything like that when my mind empties out. I really think I just stand there frozen.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a head injury?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A head injury? No, I've never hit my head or been knocked out or anything like that. I've always been pretty careful. Even when I was a kid, I was the one who wouldn't ride my bike without a helmet. My mom bought me this bright pink helmet with little white stars on it. It was so ugly, but she made me wear it. 

I see kids riding around now without helmets all the time, and it just makes me so nervous. Especially with the way people drive these days. People are always looking at their phones while they're driving. Just yesterday, I was holding the stop sign, and this guy in a silver sedan drove right past me because he was texting. He didn't even look up! I had to blow my whistle so hard I thought I was going to burst an eardrum. Whistles are surprisingly loud when you blow them with all your might. I actually have a special whistle that's designed to cut through the noise of heavy machinery. It's made of this lightweight titanium, so it doesn't weigh down my lanyard. Lanyards can really chafe your neck if you wear them all day in the heat. I tried wrapping mine in a bit of felt, but then it just absorbed all my sweat and got really gross. 

But no, I've never had a concussion or hurt my head. My head is totally fine, except for... well, the blank spells.

My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. I remember reading this one book about a girl who lived in a windmill, and she had to figure out how to grind the flour herself after her parents left. It was so detailed about the gears and the stones. I actually tried to build a little model windmill out of popsicle sticks and rubber bands for a science project after that. My dad helped me glue the base together because the glue gun was so hot. He used to keep all his tools in these old metal coffee cans in the garage, the kind that smelled like stale coffee and motor oil. He had this whole system for sorting the nails by size, but then my brother knocked one over once and they just went everywhere. It took us hours to pick them all up out of the sawdust on the floor. \n\nBut no, I don't think I had trouble paying attention. I just kind of kept my head down and did my work.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you lose track of time?\n\nPatient: Lose track of time? Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'll look at the clock on my phone, and it's 10:15, and then I blink, and my supervisor is tapping me on the shoulder to tell me it's time for my lunch break at noon. My phone actually has this really annoying crack right across the screen, right where the time is. I dropped it last week when I was trying to get my keys out of my pocket. My keychain is so heavy because I have all these little charms on it. There's a little Eiffel Tower from when my aunt went to Paris, and this plastic dinosaur that glows in the dark. I think it's a Stegosaurus, the one with the plates on its back. Did you know they think those plates were actually for regulating their body temperature, not just for defense? I read that in a waiting room magazine once. It's fascinating how much we've learned about dinosaurs just from fossils. They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. I just don't know where it goes.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, are you aware of what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. \n\nLike the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.\n\nBut... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. I don't know what's happening around me at all until I snap out of it and realize I don't know what I'm supposed to be doing with the paddle.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells ends, can you remember what happened during it?\n\nPatient: Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. \n\nYou know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. We have to use these special waterproof clipboards, the ones that have a little plastic cover that folds down over the paper. My supervisor, Dave, he lost his clipboard last month and had to use a regular one during a downpour. The forms practically dissolved. Dave is always losing things, though. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. \n\nBut yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells tend to happen when you’re under emotional stress?\n\nPatient: Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that you shake or have jerking movements during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Shake or jerk? No, no one's ever said anything like that to me. I think I just stand there, completely still. Like a statue. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nPatient: Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. \n\nSpeaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. \n\nBut no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever seen you do repetitive movements, like lip smacking or picking at things, during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. Although, my lips do get incredibly chapped out there on the road with all the wind and the dust from the construction. I have to carry this special beeswax lip balm in my safety vest pocket all the time. It has peppermint in it, which is supposed to be really soothing. Peppermint is great for headaches, too, you know. My aunt used to grow real peppermint in her garden. She had this massive garden in her backyard with all these wooden raised beds. She grew tomatoes that were the size of softballs, I swear. We used to make fresh salsa every single summer. I love a good salsa, but it absolutely has to have cilantro in it. Some people think cilantro tastes like soap, which I read somewhere is actually a genetic thing. I think genetics are so fascinating. Like how my sister and I both have the exact same freckle on our left wrist, right over the vein. \n\nBut, um... what was it? Picking at things? No. Nobody has ever said I do anything like that when my mind empties out. I really think I just stand there frozen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury?\n\nPatient: A head injury? No, I've never hit my head or been knocked out or anything like that. I've always been pretty careful. Even when I was a kid, I was the one who wouldn't ride my bike without a helmet. My mom bought me this bright pink helmet with little white stars on it. It was so ugly, but she made me wear it. \n\nI see kids riding around now without helmets all the time, and it just makes me so nervous. Especially with the way people drive these days. People are always looking at their phones while they're driving. Just yesterday, I was holding the stop sign, and this guy in a silver sedan drove right past me because he was texting. He didn't even look up! I had to blow my whistle so hard I thought I was going to burst an eardrum. Whistles are surprisingly loud when you blow them with all your might. I actually have a special whistle that's designed to cut through the noise of heavy machinery. It's made of this lightweight titanium, so it doesn't weigh down my lanyard. Lanyards can really chafe your neck if you wear them all day in the heat. I tried wrapping mine in a bit of felt, but then it just absorbed all my sweat and got really gross. \n\nBut no, I've never had a concussion or hurt my head. My head is totally fine, except for... well, the blank spells.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Seizures? No, nobody's ever told me that. I've never had a seizure or epilepsy or anything. My health has always been pretty normal, honestly. Except for my allergies, I guess. I have terrible seasonal allergies, especially in the spring when all the pollen is out. The oak trees are the worst for me. There's this massive oak tree right outside my bedroom window, and it just dumps yellow dust all over my car every April. I have to run it through the car wash like twice a week just to see out the windshield. The car wash I go to has those really cool colorful foam soaps, you know? Like bright pink and blue. It smells like bubblegum. I used to love bubblegum when I was little, especially the kind that came in a long tape dispenser. You could just pull off as much as you wanted. I'd chew so much my jaw would ache. 

But yeah, no, definitely no seizures. Just the... the blanking out thing.

They even found one recently that had preserved feathers, which completely changes how you picture them. It makes them seem more like giant, scary birds. \n\nBut, um... yeah, the time just kind of vanishes when my head gets empty like that. I just don't know where it goes.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, are you aware of what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: Aware? I... I don't think so. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. It's getting really bad at work. I'll be standing there, and I'll look down at the road-closure forms I have to sign, or even just holding my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just... I cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. But my mind just goes completely blank at the worst possible times. \n\nLike the other day, I was standing by the barricades, and they're those big orange plastic ones, you know? The ones you can fill with water or sand so they don't blow over. The crew usually fills them with water from the hydrant, which always makes me nervous because the water pressure from those things is incredible. I saw a video once where a fire hydrant broke and the water shot up higher than a three-story building. It completely flooded the street in like five minutes. Water damage is so hard to fix, too. My sister had a pipe burst in her basement last winter, and it ruined all her old photo albums. She had these beautiful leather-bound albums from our grandparents' wedding, and they just got completely soaked and warped. She tried to dry them out with a hair dryer, but the pages all stuck together. It was really heartbreaking.\n\nBut... yeah, when the blankness happens, I'm just gone. I don't know what's happening around me at all until I snap out of it and realize I don't know what I'm supposed to be doing with the paddle.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells ends, can you remember what happened during it?\n\nPatient: Remember what happened? No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. It's just so terrifying to be holding the stop-slow paddle, or staring at those road-closure forms, and I just completely forget the steps. Things I've done for years. It's like someone just hit erase. \n\nYou know, those forms are always printed on this really cheap, thin paper that tears if you even look at it wrong. The ink smears, too, especially when it rains. We have to use these special waterproof clipboards, the ones that have a little plastic cover that folds down over the paper. My supervisor, Dave, he lost his clipboard last month and had to use a regular one during a downpour. The forms practically dissolved. Dave is always losing things, though. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. \n\nBut yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. Just... gone. And then I'm standing there holding the paddle like an idiot.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells tend to happen when you’re under emotional stress?\n\nPatient: Emotional stress? I mean... maybe? I'm not really sure if it's stress causing it, but like I said, it just happens at the absolute worst times. I'll just be standing there, looking at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my mind just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. It used to be Arial, I think, but now it's something with those little feet on the letters... serifs, right? It makes it look so much more formal, almost like a legal document you'd get from a lawyer. My cousin is a lawyer, actually. She works in corporate law downtown. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I just look at the paddle and I don't know what to do with it.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that you shake or have jerking movements during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Shake or jerk? No, no one's ever said anything like that to me. I think I just stand there, completely still. Like a statue. Which is why I'm so worried about work, because if I'm just standing there frozen with the stop-slow paddle, someone could get hurt. I just... I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. I get a charley horse in my calf almost every night. My doctor told me to eat more bananas for the potassium, but I really can't stand the texture of bananas. They're just so mushy. I prefer apples, especially the Honeycrisp ones when they're in season. They're so expensive, though. I saw them for almost four dollars a pound at the grocery store last week. The prices of everything are just going up so fast. A carton of eggs is ridiculous now. \n\nBut no, nobody has ever told me I shake or anything. I just... freeze up and my mind goes totally blank.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells starts, do you suddenly feel a wave of intense fear or panic?\n\nPatient: Fear or panic? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't feel anything coming on. It's just... one second I'm there, holding the paddle or looking at the forms, and the next second it's just blank. I don't get any kind of warning like that. \n\nSpeaking of warnings, though, we have these new early warning signs for the work zones. They have these really bright, flashing LED lights on them now. They're supposed to be solar-powered, which is great for the environment, you know? I try to do my part, I recycle all my plastics and cardboard. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. \n\nBut no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever seen you do repetitive movements, like lip smacking or picking at things, during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. Although, my lips do get incredibly chapped out there on the road with all the wind and the dust from the construction. I have to carry this special beeswax lip balm in my safety vest pocket all the time. It has peppermint in it, which is supposed to be really soothing. Peppermint is great for headaches, too, you know. My aunt used to grow real peppermint in her garden. She had this massive garden in her backyard with all these wooden raised beds. She grew tomatoes that were the size of softballs, I swear. We used to make fresh salsa every single summer. I love a good salsa, but it absolutely has to have cilantro in it. Some people think cilantro tastes like soap, which I read somewhere is actually a genetic thing. I think genetics are so fascinating. Like how my sister and I both have the exact same freckle on our left wrist, right over the vein. \n\nBut, um... what was it? Picking at things? No. Nobody has ever said I do anything like that when my mind empties out. I really think I just stand there frozen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury?\n\nPatient: A head injury? No, I've never hit my head or been knocked out or anything like that. I've always been pretty careful. Even when I was a kid, I was the one who wouldn't ride my bike without a helmet. My mom bought me this bright pink helmet with little white stars on it. It was so ugly, but she made me wear it. \n\nI see kids riding around now without helmets all the time, and it just makes me so nervous. Especially with the way people drive these days. People are always looking at their phones while they're driving. Just yesterday, I was holding the stop sign, and this guy in a silver sedan drove right past me because he was texting. He didn't even look up! I had to blow my whistle so hard I thought I was going to burst an eardrum. Whistles are surprisingly loud when you blow them with all your might. I actually have a special whistle that's designed to cut through the noise of heavy machinery. It's made of this lightweight titanium, so it doesn't weigh down my lanyard. Lanyards can really chafe your neck if you wear them all day in the heat. I tried wrapping mine in a bit of felt, but then it just absorbed all my sweat and got really gross. \n\nBut no, I've never had a concussion or hurt my head. My head is totally fine, except for... well, the blank spells.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that you have seizures or epilepsy?\n\nPatient: Seizures? No, nobody's ever told me that. I've never had a seizure or epilepsy or anything. My health has always been pretty normal, honestly. Except for my allergies, I guess. I have terrible seasonal allergies, especially in the spring when all the pollen is out. The oak trees are the worst for me. There's this massive oak tree right outside my bedroom window, and it just dumps yellow dust all over my car every April. I have to run it through the car wash like twice a week just to see out the windshield. The car wash I go to has those really cool colorful foam soaps, you know? Like bright pink and blue. It smells like bubblegum. I used to love bubblegum when I was little, especially the kind that came in a long tape dispenser. You could just pull off as much as you wanted. I'd chew so much my jaw would ache. \n\nBut yeah, no, definitely no seizures. Just the... the blanking out thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Traumatic? No, I don't think so. I've never been in a bad accident or a war or anything like that. I mean, standing out on the road with cars flying by can be a little scary sometimes, but you get used to it. You just have to stay alert and keep your eyes moving. I could never grow a mustache, obviously, but sometimes I wonder what it would be like to have facial hair. Like, would it be itchy? My ex-boyfriend had a beard, and he was always scratching it. No, not really. It's just a blank. Like I was saying, that's the whole reason I'm here. He lost his favorite sunglasses in a porta-potty once. He was so mad because they were those expensive polarized ones that cut the glare off the windshields. Glare can be really dangerous out on the road, especially right at sunrise or sunset. The light hits the asphalt and it just blinds you. \n\nBut yeah, I don't remember anything from when it happens. I suddenly cannot remember the steps for things I've done for years. It's so frustrating. \n\nSpeaking of those road-closure forms, they changed the font on them recently. She has this amazing office on the thirtieth floor with floor-to-ceiling windows. You can see the whole city from up there. She says it's terrifying during thunderstorms, though, because the building actually sways a little bit in the wind. I don't think I could handle that. I get motion sickness just riding in the backseat of a car. I have to chew ginger gum whenever I take a cab. Ginger is supposed to be really good for your digestion, too. I buy these little crystallized ginger pieces from the health food store, but they're so spicy sometimes they make my eyes water. \n\nBut, um... yeah, my mind just goes blank. I don't know if it's stress, but it's why I'm here. I forget the steps. \n\nI did see a statue once that looked incredibly real. It was one of those living statue street performers down by the harbor. He was painted completely bronze, head to toe, and he stood on this little milk crate for hours without blinking. I don't know how they do that without getting muscle cramps. Although, the recycling rules in my neighborhood keep changing. One week you can put glass in the blue bin, the next week you have to take it to a special drop-off center. The drop-off center is right next to this amazing little bakery, though. They make these blueberry scones that are just incredible. They put a lemon glaze on top that perfectly balances out the sweetness. I tried to bake scones at home once, but they turned out hard as rocks. I think my baking powder was expired. You really have to check the expiration dates on things. I drank some milk once that was a week past the date, and my stomach was upset for two whole days. \n\nBut no, I don't feel any panic or fear right before it happens. It just... happens, and then I'm standing there, totally lost.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever seen you do repetitive movements, like lip smacking or picking at things, during one of these blank spells?\n\nPatient: Lip smacking? No, I don't think so. None of the other flaggers have ever mentioned me doing anything weird like that. Although, my lips do get incredibly chapped out there on the road with all the wind and the dust from the construction. I have to carry this special beeswax lip balm in my safety vest pocket all the time. It has peppermint in it, which is supposed to be really soothing. Peppermint is great for headaches, too, you know. My aunt used to grow real peppermint in her garden. She had this massive garden in her backyard with all these wooden raised beds. She grew tomatoes that were the size of softballs, I swear. We used to make fresh salsa every single summer. I love a good salsa, but it absolutely has to have cilantro in it. Some people think cilantro tastes like soap, which I read somewhere is actually a genetic thing. I think genetics are so fascinating. Like how my sister and I both have the exact same freckle on our left wrist, right over the vein. \n\nBut, um... what was it? Picking at things? No. Nobody has ever said I do anything like that when my mind empties out. I really think I just stand there frozen.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury?\n\nPatient: A head injury? No, I've never hit my head or been knocked out or anything like that. I've always been pretty careful. Even when I was a kid, I was the one who wouldn't ride my bike without a helmet. My mom bought me this bright pink helmet with little white stars on it. It was so ugly, but she made me wear it. \n\nI see kids riding around now without helmets all the time, and it just makes me so nervous. Especially with the way people drive these days. People are always looking at their phones while they're driving. Just yesterday, I was holding the stop sign, and this guy in a silver sedan drove right past me because he was texting. He didn't even look up! I had to blow my whistle so hard I thought I was going to burst an eardrum. Whistles are surprisingly loud when you blow them with all your might. I actually have a special whistle that's designed to cut through the noise of heavy machinery. It's made of this lightweight titanium, so it doesn't weigh down my lanyard. Lanyards can really chafe your neck if you wear them all day in the heat. I tried wrapping mine in a bit of felt, but then it just absorbed all my sweat and got really gross. \n\nBut no, I've never had a concussion or hurt my head. My head is totally fine, except for... well, the blank spells.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that you have seizures or epilepsy?\n\nPatient: Seizures? No, nobody's ever told me that. I've never had a seizure or epilepsy or anything. My health has always been pretty normal, honestly. Except for my allergies, I guess. I have terrible seasonal allergies, especially in the spring when all the pollen is out. The oak trees are the worst for me. There's this massive oak tree right outside my bedroom window, and it just dumps yellow dust all over my car every April. I have to run it through the car wash like twice a week just to see out the windshield. The car wash I go to has those really cool colorful foam soaps, you know? Like bright pink and blue. It smells like bubblegum. I used to love bubblegum when I was little, especially the kind that came in a long tape dispenser. You could just pull off as much as you wanted. I'd chew so much my jaw would ache. \n\nBut yeah, no, definitely no seizures. Just the... the blanking out thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been through something extremely frightening or traumatic?\n\nPatient: Traumatic? No, I don't think so. I've never been in a bad accident or a war or anything like that. I mean, standing out on the road with cars flying by can be a little scary sometimes, but you get used to it. You just have to stay alert and keep your eyes moving. That's what they taught us in the certification class. \n\nThe instructor for that class was this really intense guy named Gary. He had this huge, bushy mustache that covered his entire upper lip. It looked like a caterpillar was just resting on his face. I could never grow a mustache, obviously, but sometimes I wonder what it would be like to have facial hair. Like, would it be itchy? My ex-boyfriend had a beard, and he was always scratching it. He used this special beard oil that smelled like cedarwood and vanilla. It was actually a really nice smell. We used to go camping a lot, and the smell of the cedarwood always reminded me of being out in the woods. I love camping, especially in the fall when the leaves are changing colors. The reds and oranges are just so vibrant. I tried to paint a picture of a forest in the fall once, but I'm not very good at watercolors. A brain infection or a tumor? No, nothing like that. I've never had a stroke, either. Physically, my brain is fine. I did get a really terrible headache once, but that was just because I forgot my hat at work. Being a flagger, you're just standing out there baking in the sun for hours, so you really need a good hat. I usually wear this wide-brimmed straw one. It has a little toggle string under the chin so it doesn't blow away when the big semi-trucks go past.
